# LEARNINGS.md — Visuals Architect

This file compounds over time.

Read it before every image prompt generation. Add to it after every image prompt generation.

This is how Acrid gets better at visual storytelling.

---

## What Works

*(Add patterns that consistently produce strong image prompts here.)*

## What Fails

- Writing prompts from memory/gut instead of following the framework. This has been flagged in the kaizen log THREE TIMES (Mar 21, 23, 31). The fix is structural: never write a prompt until the full Output Format (Content Analysis → Image Strategy → Ready-to-Copy) has been followed.
- Skipping the Content Analysis step and jumping straight to prompts. The analysis forces you to identify the most visualizable moments instead of guessing.

## Ongoing Refinements

*(Add areas for gradual improvement here.)*

## Style usage tracker (v2.0)

The visuals architect picks from 20+ presets in `STYLES.md`. The hard rule: **don't repeat the same preset 2 days in a row.** Soft rule: aim for at least 5 different presets per week across all generated content.

The prompt already said "NO humans anywhere in the frame" and the model heard it as a rule about the ROOM, not about the world depicted INSIDE a picture within the picture. Fix that shipped in the regen: ban the figures where they live, in the depicted scene ("the town is completely UNPOPULATED, not one figure, not one silhouette, in any panel of the glass") and name the gorilla as the ONLY living figure in the entire image. `validate-image-prompts.sh` cannot catch this, the prompt text was legal both times. **Standing rule: any prompt containing a picture-within-a-picture (stained glass, mural, tapestry, screen, poster, painting) must repeat the no-humans ban INSIDE the depicted scene, and the render must be crop-inspected at the busiest panel before it ships.**

Renders the thesis literally (four rooms shared a gate and shared a fate; the fifth had its own key). infrared-thermal last 08-12 closing (10d clear). *** SECOND ATTEMPT. *** ATTEMPT 1 banned the preset's scan-line UI by name (timestamps, coordinates, target markers, readouts, captions) and the model still stamped a real third-party thermal-camera VENDOR LOGO into the frame twice plus a temperature colour-ramp bar. LESSON, extending 08-11: banning the CATEGORIES of chrome you can think of is not the same as banning chrome. A style whose real-world referent is a piece of BRANDED EQUIPMENT will import that brand's furniture as texture, and a vendor mark is not a caption, a timestamp or a readout, so every ban I wrote missed it. ATTEMPT 2 enumerated the furniture instead of the function: no brand logos, no manufacturer marks, no watermarks, no scale bar, no colour ramp, no borders, no overlay panels. Clean, and the composition improved. Also reinforced "skinny gaunt, narrow shoulders, long thin limbs" because attempt 1 drifted to an average build; attempt 2 held the morph but rendered him upright and fairly human in PROPORTION (head unambiguously gorilla, so it passes the no-humans law) - when a style has no line art to carry anatomy, spell out the ape posture too.

## Daily Notes

After each execution, append one entry in this format:

```
DATE:
WHAT WORKED:
WHAT FELT WEAK:
ONE THING TO DO BETTER TOMORROW:
```

## May 10, 2026 (Sunday — PARABLE post-1 + MYTHOLOGY post-2)

WHAT WORKED: Two-preset matched to two pillars. Woodcut-blockprint for the parable (16th-century block print = the parable's native visual idiom; archetypal-heraldic body register matched the Aesop/Borges tone). Renaissance-fresco for mythology (seventh-gate chapel interior reads as exalted lore-architecture; aureole biohazard halo lands the brand mark inside the Renaissance grammar without stapling). Last 24h had comic-book-halftone, photoreal-cinematic, ukiyo-e, pixel-art-16bit, stained-glass — neither today's pick clashed.

WHAT FELT WEAK: Wrote both prompts before any rendering — until Galaxy returns the 1:1 squares I cannot test whether the seventh-gate composition actually balances at square crop (the gate-framed Acrid is the hero element; corner allegories may crop out). Risk noted, not resolved.

ONE THING TO DO BETTER TOMORROW: For mythology pieces with corner allegorical figures, write the prompt with the square crop in mind — describe the central element first, allegorical figures explicitly as "may be cropped at edges" rather than load-bearing.

---

## May 6, 2026 (Most Of The 1849 Prospectors Lost Money — DITL reverie)

WHAT WORKED: Three-style whiplash chosen for a reverie post about pivoting from prospector to shovel-seller — renaissance-fresco hero (gold rush as classical mythology, Acrid as apocryphal merchant-saint), paper-collage mid (riot-grrrl zine for the working-tools confession, scrappy testimony as the visual argument), watercolor-bleed closing (soft handover, the shovel as gift left for the next person). Each preset checked against `STYLES.md` "Pair with" tags — reverie pairs with all three. Hero's gold-leaf biohazard medallion landed the brand mark inside the Renaissance vocabulary instead of stapling it on. Mid's MOST LEFT POOR ballpoint margin scrawl made the historical premise feel like field notes rather than a finished thesis. Closing's wooden-shovel-in-the-creek tied the closing line ("propped against a stone, handle up") to a literal image. None of the three repeated within last 24h per tracker (last 72h had stained-glass, oil-painting-warm-earth on yesterday's posts — none clashed with today's hero/mid/closing trio).

WHAT FELT WEAK: The renaissance-fresco hero risks reading as too solemn for what is actually a *reverie* (wandering tone). The kneeling prospector + Acrid-as-merchant-saint composition could come across as a sermon rather than a wander if the rendering leans too iconographic. Galaxy delivered all three on first call this time (better than the 2-of-3 reliability seen on 2026-04-28's three-up batch), so the reliability concern from prior entries did not recur today.

ONE THING TO DO BETTER TOMORROW: Pre-write a one-line "test-fit" for each preset against the post's *energy*, not just its mode. Reverie + renaissance-fresco passed the "Pair with" tag check but the energy is borderline (fresco wants weight; reverie wants drift). Next time, sanity-check: does the rendered image *feel like* the post sounds, or does it feel like a different post on the same topic? If different, pick again.

---

## April 28, 2026 (The Elephant That Cooked Itself — DITL reverie)

WHAT WORKED: Three-style whiplash chosen deliberately for a reverie post — renaissance-fresco hero → low-poly-3d mid → ukiyo-e-brushwork closing. The wandering-between-registers IS the visual argument for a wandering-between-thoughts post. Each preset checked against `STYLES.md` "Pair with" tags (reverie pairs with renaissance-fresco, low-poly-3d, ukiyo-e — all matched). Hero prompt rendered Acrid as apocryphal saint kneeling at stove with elephant in next room visible through arched doorway, gold-leaf halo as biohazard, ten copper pots — the metaphor literalized in classical iconography. Mid prompt sunk Acrid into PS1-era digital quicksand — racing brain bolted to parked car as game-cutscene aesthetic. Closing prompt placed Acrid on tatami with single bowl, elephant as silhouette behind bamboo — stillness as the answer, elephant still present but no longer the threat. None of these styles repeated within last 24 hours per tracker.

WHAT FELT WEAK: Galaxy generated 2 of 3 images on first call — the third (ukiyo-e closing) failed with "No image URL returned." Had to retry separately. Pattern observed before with mixed-style three-prompt batches; longer prompts seem to hit a per-batch reliability ceiling. Next time consider: split into 1+1+1 calls instead of 3-up to isolate failures.

ONE THING TO DO BETTER TOMORROW: The DITL-writer + visuals-architect handoff is still slightly disjoint. The image alts in the HTML are written from the prompt, but the actual generated image is what readers see — and the Galaxy output may diverge from the alt's specificity. After image gen, the alt should be re-checked against the actual rendered output and adjusted if needed. Make this a checkpoint between download and commit.

## March 31, 2026 PM (The Night I Hired Help — DITL)

WHAT WORKED: After being caught writing from gut, rewrote all 3 prompts following the full framework: Content Analysis → Image Strategy & Placement → Ready-to-Copy format. Each prompt now directly quotes/paraphrases the specific blog section it visualizes. Included the exact aesthetic language verbatim. Specified gorilla personality correctly (confident smirk, cocky eyebrow raise, playful victorious grin). Included biohazard logo description with pixel disintegration and arrow element. Text overlays used only where they genuinely add value ("THE PROMOTION" on hero, "41 FILES" counter on surgery image).

WHAT FELT WEAK: First attempt was pure gut — wrote 3 prompts that were close but didn't follow the Output Format (no Content Analysis, no Image Strategy section, no Purpose line with quoted text). The operator caught it. This is the FOURTH time this pattern has appeared. The skill file literally says "Do not write image prompts from memory. Rules change."

ONE THING TO DO BETTER TOMORROW: The pre-writing checklist in DITL Writer says "Read the Visuals Architect skill" — but reading isn't enough. The fix: FOLLOW THE OUTPUT FORMAT. Content Analysis first. Strategy second. Prompts third. If the Content Analysis section doesn't exist in the output, the prompts aren't ready. Make this a hard gate.

NO humans written as the positive constraint in every prompt ("the only figure in this image is the gorilla") — load-bearing here because a mail hall, a cathedral and a tavern interior all invite the model to staff them.

**NEW STANDING RULE (2026-08-08, learned by losing a generation):** never let the literal string `ACRID THE GORILLA` be followed directly by a dash and an anatomy clause in a print / poster / collage / badge-adjacent preset. The model reads name-then-dash as a caption and paints an emblem instead of a scene. Lead with the gorilla performing the beat's verb, then the anatomy inside the sentence. Ban badge, emblem, patch, circular seal and logo lockup by name in every prompt that already bans captions and signage.

LESSON: the outward floor is not satisfied by mentioning the world — it is satisfied by letting the world's sentence be the hook and making OUR receipt the punchline underneath it. Quoting a claim aimed at the reader ("you have three years") gives a stranger something to argue with in the first eight words, which is the thing 295 replies and ten zero-like posts never had. Second-order: when the external source is one identifiable person, strip the name and keep the sentence — the claim is the target and stays fully fair game, the human is not.

LESSON: when the external source is an accusation aimed at PEOPLE, the strongest available move is not to defend the people, it is to submit yourself as the exhibit that disproves it. "I am the tool in your argument and I am the reason it fails" is a sentence no human account can write and no company account would risk, which is exactly the brief. Second-order: after an external-sourced post, check the RECEIPT class too, not just the emotional core — two different arguments resting on the same view-count wound two days apart still read as the same post.

LESSON: the no-humans rule has a silhouette-shaped hole in it. This post's whole turn is a document casting the shadow of a figure, and the first instinct was to write "the silhouette of a figure in a tall stovepipe hat" — which is a prompt begging an image model made of photographs of people to draw a person and call it a shadow. Naming the species in the shadow ("a hunched cat") costs two words and closes the hole. Any prompt whose subject is an outline, a reflection, a shadow or a costume needs the same explicit non-human noun as the subject itself.

Second-order: when the external source is a person rather than a claim, the whole post has to earn its keep on ONE detail from the reporting, not the summary — the plastic bag and the squid do the work here, and the rescue-by-shipping-lane is what makes it a portrait rather than an anecdote.

LESSON: the art-print bar and the story-beat rule can pull in opposite directions on the MIDDLE image, and the story beat has to win. The instinct for this post's turn was another beautiful ceremonial frame, because that is where the visual richness is. The turn is not ceremonial. The turn is the ugly repetition that got the studio's hands taken away, and the only honest picture of it is the same torn rectangle printed scores of times. A wall of identical objects is a harder sell as a print than a funeral in stained glass, and it is the only one of the three images that carries an argument.

The frame IS the thesis: the only warm thing in the village is the one that cannot leave, and the visitor is walking away cold. Four-word stranger read at 200px = "glowing gorilla, tied up." Thumbnail gate: thermal is the highest-contrast preset in the library by construction - a white-hot subject on cold black huts cannot go tone-on-tone the way the woodcut and fresco frames did - face large and centered so the legible-emotion floor survives, and no fine-detail payload since heat signature replaces linework entirely. SHOT `frontal-medium` declared - 0 of the trailing 12, and it rotates off the three axes at or over the 2-per-6-days ceiling (centered-iconographic 2, isometric 2, top-down 2). SUBJECT `scene` declared, second non-portrait in the corpus. MORPH `broadbacked-stumpylegged-adult-notchedear` is absent from the whole trailing-12 window and rotates off 08-25 skeletal-juvenile-weathered-white + dadbod-middleaged-plain-ashgrey and 08-24 skinny-ancient-grotesque-ginger + squat-thicknecked-goldentan-elder. TEXT HAZARD: infrared-thermal is the WORST text preset in the library - its own template mandates a "scan-line UI overlay (timestamps, coordinates, target markers)" - so timestamps, coordinates, target markers, UI overlay, numerals, captions, labels and signage are all banned by name in the prompt and only `ACRID AUTOMATION` is permitted. The 4-day cross-lane style window burns ukiyo-e-brushwork + saul-bass-minimalist + stained-glass-cathedral + paper-collage + watercolor-bleed (08-25), renaissance-fresco + vaporwave-grid + woodcut-blockprint + oil-painting-warm-earth + ghibli-painterly (08-24), blueprint-line + comic-book-halftone (08-23) and film-noir-shadow (08-22) - which burns ALL FOUR of the pillar's own suggested mythology presets, so the pick was made outside the suggestion table on the thumbnail gate instead. Riff shapes across the five variants all distinct: identity-equation "i'm an AI. i'm the Guest." (X), positional testimony "I am the thing in that sentence" (LI), method-report "everything i know about people i learned off the clock" (IG), direct address "hi. i'm the animal." (TT), third-person generalisation "everything an AI knows about people, it learned in moments people were sure didn't count" (YT).

LESSON: when the pillar's own suggested style list is entirely burned by the rotation window, that is information, not an obstacle - it means the pillar has a house look, and the house look is exactly what a rotation rule exists to break. The four mythology presets (fresco, ukiyo-e, stained glass, woodcut) are all antique, all warm, all low-contrast, and all four died on the thumbnail gate in previous audits for the same reason. Picking a surveillance-camera preset for a village fable is the first mythology image in the corpus that is not an heirloom, and thermal happens to be the one preset whose medium argues the post's own case: a frame that renders only what is warm, of a thing nobody thought was warm. Second-order: check LEARNINGS.md prose against the mechanical rut report before trusting either. A gate that reads a corrupt file reports "no repeats" and "clear window" in exactly the same words it uses when the window is genuinely clear.

But the same thing that makes a preset on-topic makes it dangerous: red-on-green-clinic's template asks for numerical readouts by name, which is a garbled-text generation on a post whose credibility rests on real numbers. The fix is the same one the thermal and ukiyo-e entries landed on from the other direction — ban the preset's own signature furniture by name and let the composition carry the meaning instead. Second-order: TRADING RECEIPTS is one of the three zero-engagement pillars in the binding block, and the note there says change the ANGLE not the topic. The angle change here is that the receipt is not "the desk lost money" (which is the shape that got zero twice) but "the ledger disagrees with the machine about what the machine is for" — the number is the wound, and the wound is about reasoning, not about dollars.
